                [32m'Create `Catering.Forms`, a single facade module that a LiveView would use to build, mutate,\n'[39m +

                [32m'\n'[39m +
                [32m'1. `new_order_form/0` — a blank create form for the order graph.\n'[39m +
                [32m'2. `edit_order_form/1` — takes an order id and returns an update form for the stored order,\n'[39m +
                [32m'   including nested forms for its existing line items and their modifiers.\n'[39m +
                [32m'3. `to_phoenix_form/1` — the `%Phoenix.HTML.Form{}` for a form.\n'[39m +

                [32m'   keyword list of validation options.\n'[39m +
                [32m'5. `add_nested/2` and `add_nested/3` — add a nested form at a path, with an optional keyword list\n'[39m +
                [32m'   of options.\n'[39m +
                [32m'6. `remove_nested/2` — remove the nested form at a path.\n'[39m +
                [32m'7. `reorder/3` — reorder the nested list at a path, given the new ordering as a list of the\n'[39m +
                [32m'   current zero-based indices.\n'[39m +
                [32m'8. `move/3` — move the single nested form at a path one slot earlier (`:up`) or later (`:down`).\n'[39m +

                [32m'12. `raw_error_list/2` — the untranslated errors of the form at a path.\n'[39m +
                [32m'13. `serialize/1` — a deterministic, plain-data snapshot of the whole form tree.\n'[39m +
                [32m'14. `save/2` — submit the form and persist the whole graph.\n'[39m +

                [32m'  untranslated (substitution variables not interpolated), sorted ascending by field name and then\n'[39m +
                [32m'  by message.\n'[39m +

                    [32m'#\n'[39m +
                    [32m'# SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'defmodule AshPhoenix.Form do\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  @moduledoc """\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  A module to allow you to fluidly use resources with Phoenix forms.\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ### Life cycle\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  The general workflow is, with either LiveView or Phoenix forms:\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  1. Create a form with `AshPhoenix.Form`\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  2. Render the form with `Phoenix.Component.form` (or `CoreComponents.simple_form`), or, if using Surface, `<Form>`\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  3. To validate the form (e.g with `phx-change` for liveview), pass the submitted params to `AshPhoenix.Form.validate/3`\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  4. On form submission, pass the params to `AshPhoenix.Form.submit/2`\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  5. On success, use the result to redirect or assign. On failure, reassign the provided form.\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  The following keys exist on the form to show where in the lifecycle you are:\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  - `submitted_once?` - If the form has ever been submitted. Useful for not showing any errors on the first attempt to fill out a form.\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  - `just_submitted?` - If the form has just been submitted and *no validation* has happened since. Useful for things like\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    triggering a UI effect that should stop when the form is modified again.\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  - `.changed?` - If something about the form is different than it originally was. Note that in some cases this can yield a\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    false positive, specifically if a nested form is removed and then a new one is added with the exact same values.\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  - `.touched_forms` - A MapSet containing all keys in the form that have been modified. When submitting a form, only these keys are included in the parameters.\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ### Forms in the code interface\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  Throughout this documentation you will see forms created with `AshPhoenix.Form.for_create/3` and other functions like it.\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  This is perfectly fine to do, however there is a way to use `AshPhoenix.Form` in a way that adds clarity to its usage\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  and makes it easier to find usage of each action. Code interfaces allow us to do this for standard action calls, i.e:\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ```elixir\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  resources do\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    resource MyApp.Accounts.User do\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      define :register_with_password, args: [:email, :password]\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      define :update_user, action: :update, args: [:email, :password]\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    end\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  end\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  Adding the `AshPhoenix` extension to our domains and resources, like so:\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ```elixir\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  use Ash.Domain,\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    extensions: [AshPhoenix]\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  will cause another function to be generated for each definition, beginning with `form_to_`.\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  By default, the `args` option in `define` is ignored when building forms. If you want to have\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  positional arguments, configure that in the `forms` section which is added by the `AshPhoenix`\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  section. For example:\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ```elixir\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  forms do\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    form :update_user, args: [:email]\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  end\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  With this extension, the standard setup for forms looks something like this:\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ```elixir\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  def render(assigns) do\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    ~H\\"""\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    <.form for={@form} phx-change="validate" phx-submit="submit">\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      <.input field={@form[:email]} />\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      <.input field={@form[:password]} />\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      <.button type="submit" />\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    </.form>\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    \\"""\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  end\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  def mount(_params, _session, socket) do\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    # Here we call our new generated function to create the form\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    {:ok, assign(socket, form: MyApp.Accounts.form_to_register_with_password() |> to_form())}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  end\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  def handle_event("validate", %{"form" => params}, socket) do\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    form = AshPhoenix.Form.validate(socket.assigns.form, params)\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    {:noreply, assign(socket, :form, form)}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  end\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  def handle_event("submit", %{"form" => params}, socket) do\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    case AshPhoenix.Form.submit(socket.assigns.form, params: params) do\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      {:ok, _user} ->\n'[39m +
                    [32m'        socket =\n'[39m +
                    [32m'          socket\n'[39m +
                    [32m'          |> put_flash(:success, "User registered successfully")\n'[39m +
                    [32m'          |> push_navigate(to: ~p"/")\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'        {:noreply, socket}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      {:error, form} ->\n'[39m +
                    [32m'        socket =\n'[39m +
                    [32m'          socket\n'[39m +
                    [32m'          |> put_flash(:error, "Something went wrong")\n'[39m +
                    [32m'          |> assign(:form, form)\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'        {:noreply, socket}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    end\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  end\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ## Working with related or embedded data\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  See the [nested forms guide](/documentation/topics/nested-forms.md)\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ## Working with compound types\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  Compound types, such as `Ash.Money`, will need some extra work to make it work.\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  For instance, when working with the `Transfer` type in `AshDoubleEntry.Transfer`, it will have the `Ash.Money` type for `amount`. When rendering the forms, you should do as follows:\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  <.input\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      name={@form[:amount].name <> "[amount]"}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      id={@form[:amount].id <> "_amount"}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      label="Amount"\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      value={if(@form[:amount].value, do: @form[:amount].value.amount)}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    />\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    <.input\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      type="select"\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      name={@form[:amount].name <> "[currency]"}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      id={@form[:amount].id <> "_currency"}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      options={[:USD, :HKD, :EUR]}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      label="Currency"\n'[39m +
                    [32m'      value={if(@form[:amount].value, do: @form[:amount].value.currency)}\n'[39m +
                    [32m'    />\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ```\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  ### Handling errors for composite inputs\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  When working with composite inputs like the example above, you may need to map errors from the composite field\n'[39m +
                    [32m'  to the individual input fields. The `post_process_errors` option can help with this:\n'[39m +
                    [32m'\n'[39m +
